Category: Cybersecurity

A critical security patch has been released for the GoBalance onion load balancer, addressing two significant vulnerabilities that could allow complete takeover of onion services. The first vulnerability involves recovering the master key from a public descriptor, while the second allows the acceptance of forged descriptors, facilitating a traffic hijack without key recovery. The patch includes a proof-of-concept and regression tests to confirm the effectiveness of the fixes.

 - A security patch for GoBalance addresses two critical vulnerabilities, one allowing master key recovery and the other enabling acceptance of forged onion service descriptors.
 - The patch includes a proof-of-concept to demonstrate the vulnerabilities and regression tests to ensure the fixes are effective.

Source: https://kitploit.com/en/tools/github/kolmteistov/gobalance-patch

The execution method and scheduling have raised logistical and legal questions, particularly given Hasan's medical condition as he is paralyzed and requires a wheelchair.

International legal experts argue that these actions fit the criteria for genocide as outlined by the Genocide Convention, yet global political will is lacking to officially designate these acts as such. The International Criminal Court is also facing significant challenges that hinder its ability to address these crimes effectively.

 - Russia has forcibly transferred at least 20,651 Ukrainian children since the start of the conflict.

 - The children face re-education in Russia and potential conscription into the military as they reach draft age.

This finding is significant as it represents one of the rare instances where a planet may exist in the habitable zone of an O-class star, though its conditions remain speculative and the likelihood of habitability is assessed at just over 50%. The discovery was made using NASA's TESS satellite, which has been instrumental in locating exoplanets by analyzing the pulsations of stars.

Zohar Pinhasi, owner of MonsterCloud, has been indicted for allegedly defrauding ransomware victims by billing them millions for data recovery solutions that were ineffective and simply involved paying the ransom fees on their behalf. The indictment states that he misled clients about the company’s capabilities, charging marked-up prices while the clients were often unaware that ransoms were being paid to cybercriminals. This case highlights fraudulent practices in the ransomware remediation industry, affecting numerous businesses across the United States and Canada.

 - Zohar Pinhasi's firm, MonsterCloud, allegedly billed clients millions for ineffective ransomware recovery services.
 - The company paid over $8 million in ransoms while charging clients more than $19 million for services.

 - Approximately 20 million barrels of Iranian oil remain unaccounted for amidst sanctions.
